\displaystyle\quad\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\lambda^{S}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{Z})+\frac{1}{2}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S})^{\top}(\mathbf{Z}+\mathbf{Z}^{\top})\boldsymbol{1}_{n}-\langle\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot},\;\mathbf{Z}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\bigl\langle\lambda^{S}\mathbf{I}_{n}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S})^{\top}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}-\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}-\mathbf{B}^{S},\;\mathbf{Z}\bigr\rangle+\langle\mathbf{B}^{S},\mathbf{Z}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\langle\mathbf{W}^{S},\mathbf{Z}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{B}^{S},\mathbf{Z}\rangle

where step
(i)
uses primal constraints
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{Z})=K
,
\mathbf{Z}^{\top}=\mathbf{Z}
and
\mathbf{Z}\boldsymbol{1}_{n}=\boldsymbol{1}_{n}
,
step
(ii)
uses
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{Z})=\langle\mathbf{I}_{n},\mathbf{Z}\rangle
, and added and subtracted
\langle\mathbf{B}^{S},\mathbf{Z}\rangle
. This duality gap is zero
if
\langle\mathbf{W}^{S},\mathbf{Z}\rangle=0
(C3) and
\langle\mathbf{B}^{S},\mathbf{Z}\rangle=0
(C4).

3. Condition (C5) from the block-diagonal structure.

Finally, we derive condition (C5), which ensures that
\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}
is the unique solution to the primal SDP. To this end, we first show that it is the only primal feasible point with the following block-diagonal structure:
where
\mathbf{Z}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}=0
for all
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
:

\mathbf{Z}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{Z}^{(1)}&\mathbf{0}&\cdots&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{Z}^{(2)}&\cdots&\mathbf{0}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{0}&\cdots&\mathbf{Z}^{(K)}\end{bmatrix}.

We use proof by contradiction.
Suppose there exists a primal feasible
\mathbf{Z}\neq\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}
with the block diagonal structure described above. By the primal constraint
\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{1}_{n}=\mathbf{1}_{n}
, each block
\mathbf{Z}^{(k)}
satisfies
\mathbf{Z}^{(k)}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}=\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}
, which implies that
(1,n_{k}^{-1/2}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|})
is an eigenvalue-eigenvector pair of
\mathbf{Z}^{(k)}
. Since the trace of a matrix equals the sum of its eigenvalues, this implies
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{Z}^{(k)})\geq 1
for every
k
.
Combining this with the overall trace constraint
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{Z})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{Z}^{(k)})=K
, we deduce that
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{Z}^{(k)})=1
for every
k
.
Additionally,
each
\mathbf{Z}^{(k)}
is positive semidefinite,
due to the primal constraint
\mathbf{Z}\succeq 0
, along with the Schur complement condition for the positive semidefiniteness of block matrices.
Then
\mathbf{Z}^{(k)}
must take the form
n_{k}^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}^{\top}
,
since
1
is the only nonzero eigenvalue of
\mathbf{Z}^{(k)}
, corresponding to the eigenvector
n_{k}^{-1/2}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}
. Consequently,
\mathbf{Z}=\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}
, a contradiction.

Given this block diagonal structure and the condition
\mathrm{tr}(\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{Z}^{\ast})=0
, we conclude that
\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}
is the unique solution to the primal SDP problem if
(C1)-(C4) is satisfied and

\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}>0
for all
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
.
This corresponds to condition (C5) and completes the proof of Lemma
13
.
∎

C.4.2
Proof of Lemma
14

The proof proceeds in three steps:

1.

Preliminary computations,

2.

Necessary condition for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}
,

3.

Necessary condition for
\mathbf{B}^{S}
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we assume that conditions (C1)–(C5) from
13
are satisfied.
Recall that true cluster membership matrix is defined as
\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}
,
where
\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}
, as defined in Section
1
, is the indicator vector for membership in
G_{k}^{\ast}
.

1. Preliminary computations.

We first summarize the results for reference. The derivations will follow.

\displaystyle\bigl((\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}\bigr)_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=|G_{l}^{\ast}|\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l},~\text{for }(k,l)\in[K]^{2}~(\text{no condition required}),

(28)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=0~\text{for }k\in[K]~(\text{by (C2) and (C3)}),

(29)

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{0}~\text{for }k\in[K]~(\text{by (C2) and (C3)}),

(30)

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{0}~\text{for }k\in[K]~(\text{by (C4)})

(31)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=0~\text{for }k\in[K]~(\text{by (C4)}),\text{and}

(32)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}~\text{for }1\leq k\neq l\leq K~(\text{no condition required}).

(33)

We start with (
28
).
For
1\leq k\leq K
and
1\leq l\leq K
,
using only the definition of
\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}
and
\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}
given in Section
1
,
we have

\displaystyle\bigl((\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}\bigr)_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}(\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}+(\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})_{G_{k}^{\ast},(G_{k}^{\ast})^{C}}(\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}})_{(G_{k}^{\ast})^{C}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}(\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})_{G_{k}^{\ast},(G_{k}^{\ast})^{C}}(\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}})_{(G_{k}^{\ast})^{C}}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}

\displaystyle=|G_{l}^{\ast}|\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}.

where step
(i)
uses
(\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=0
.

Using the definition of
\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}
and condition (C3), which states that
\langle\mathbf{W}^{S},\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}\rangle=0
, we obtain:

\langle\mathbf{W}^{S},\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\langle\mathbf{W}^{S},\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\rangle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=0.

By the condition (C2),
which states that
\mathbf{W}^{S}\succeq 0
,
we have

\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\geq 0
for all
k=1,\ldots,K
.
This along with the equality above implies (
29
):

\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=0~\text{for all}~k=1,\ldots,K.

Given that
\mathbf{W}^{S}\succeq 0
,
the function
\mathbf{v}\mapsto\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{v}
is convex and bounded below by 0.
Therefore,
\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=0

implies that
\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}
minimizes
\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{v}
. By the first order condition, we obtain (
30
)

\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{0}~\text{for all}~k=1,\ldots,K.

By condition (C4), which states that
\langle\mathbf{B}^{S},\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}\rangle=0
, and the block-diagonal structure of
\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}
,
we obtain (
31
):

\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{0},

which implies (
32
):

(\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}(\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}+\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{C}}(\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}})_{{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{C}}=0.

On the other hand, since the off-diagonal block
\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}
may be nonzero for
k\neq l
, we can only conclude as (
33
):

(\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}.

2. Necessary condition for
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}
.

Our goal is to derive condition (
14
).
Only using the definition of
\mathbf{W}^{S}
given in Lemma
13
, we compute:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S})^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}+\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}-(\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}\bigr)\mathbf{1}_{n}+\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}-(\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}.

(34)

Then the restriction of
\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}
to the indices in
G_{k}^{\ast}
is given by:

(\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\overset{(i)}{=}\lambda^{S}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}\bigr)\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}-|G_{k}^{\ast}|\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\overset{(ii)}{=}0,

(35)

where
step
(i)
uses (
28
) and (
32
),
which require (C4),
and step
(ii)
uses (
30
), which requires (C2) and (C3).
Rearranging the terms,
this computation implies:

\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=2\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}-\biggl\{\frac{2\lambda^{S}}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}\bigr)\biggr\}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}.

(36)

It remains to express
\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}
on the right-hand side solely in terms of the data and
\lambda^{S}
. To this end, we compute:

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\biggl\{\lambda^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}\bigr)\mathbf{1}_{n}+\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}-(\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\lambda^{S}|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{k}^{\ast}|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}-|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}

(37)

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{=}0,

where
step
(i)
uses (
34
)
step
(ii)
uses (
31
), which require (C4),
and
step
(iii)
uses (
29
),
which require (C2) and (C3).
Rearranging the terms,
this computation implies:

\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}=|G_{k}^{\ast}|\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-\lambda^{S}.

(38)

This with (
36
) implies:

\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=2\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}-\biggl\{\frac{\lambda^{S}}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\biggr\}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}.

This corresponds precisely to condition
14
.

3. Necessary condition for
\mathbf{B}^{S}
.

Our goal is to derive condition (
15
).
Fix
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
.
Similar to (
34
),
only using the definition of
\mathbf{W}^{S}
given in Lemma
13
,
we compute:

\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}=\lambda^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}+\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}\bigr)\mathbf{1}_{n}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}-(\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot})\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}-\mathbf{B}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}.

Then, the restriction of
\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}
to the indices in
G_{k}^{\ast}
is given by:

(\mathbf{W}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\overset{(i)}{=}\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}\bigr)\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}-|G_{l}^{\ast}|\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}-\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\overset{(ii)}{=}0,

(39)

where step
(i)
uses
(\mathbf{1}_{G_{l}^{\ast}})_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=0
,
(
28
), and (
33
),
and step
(ii)
uses (
30
),
which requires (C2) and (C3).
Rearranging the terms, this computation implies:

\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}=\frac{1}{2}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{i}\bigr)\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}-|G_{l}^{\ast}|\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}.

(40)

Using (
38
) with
k
replaced by
l
,
and
(
36
),
which require (C2) and (C3),
this computation implies:

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}=

\displaystyle\biggl\{\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\lambda^{S}-\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\biggr\}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}

\displaystyle+|G_{l}^{\ast}|\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}-|G_{l}^{\ast}|\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}.

This implies that, for
i\in G_{k}^{\ast}
, the
i
th row sum of

\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast}G_{l}^{\ast}}

is expressed as:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|})_{i}\overset{(i)}{=}

\displaystyle-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\lambda^{S}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}\|_{2}^{2}-2\mathbf{X}_{S,i}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}+\|\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle-\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-2\mathbf{X}_{S,i}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}+\|\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr),

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\lambda^{S}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr),

where step
(i)
adds and subtracts
\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\|\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}
.
This concludes the derivation of condition (
15
) and completes the proof of Lemma
14
.
∎

Remark 28
.

If we construct
\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}
and
\mathbf{B}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}
according to conditions (
14
) and (
15
), then step (ii) in (
35
) and (
39
) hold without requiring conditions (C2) and (C3), as the construction is explicitly designed to satisfy these equalities. This observation is useful in the proof of Lemma
17
, presented in Appendix
C.4.4
.

C.4.3
Proof of Lemma
16

Let
S\subset[p]
be a support set, and consider the corresponding primal SDP problem (
13
). We show that constructing

\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}(\lambda^{S})
,
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}(\lambda^{S})
and
\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}(\lambda^{S})

as in Lemma
15
guarantees the following:

1.

Conditions (C3) and (C4) in Lemma
13
are satisfied for any
\lambda^{S}
,

2.

Conditions (C1) and (C5) are satisfied for
\lambda^{S}<U^{S}(\mathbf{X})
.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, we omit the notation
\lambda^{S}
from

\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}(\lambda^{S})
,
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}(\lambda^{S})
, and
\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}(\lambda^{S})

and denote them

\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}
,
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}
, and
\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}
, respectively.

1. Conditions (C3) and (C4).

Condition (C4) follows directly from the block-diagonal structure of both
\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}
and
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}
.
To verify condition (C3), which requires that
\langle\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S},\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}\rangle=0
, we use the definition
\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}
to expand the inner product as

\displaystyle\langle\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S},\mathbf{Z}^{\ast}\rangle=\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}.

Thus, it suffices to verify that
\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=0
for all
k=1,\ldots,K
.
Since our construction sets

\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{0}
,
we can invoke (
37
) to state that

\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=\lambda^{S}|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{k}^{\ast}|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigl(\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}(\lambda_{S})\bigr)_{i}-|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}.

Now using our construction of
\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}(\lambda_{S})
, recalled below:

\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=2\mathbf{X}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}-\frac{\lambda^{S}}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}\|_{2}^{2}\mathbf{1}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|},

we can conclude:

\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=\lambda^{S}|G_{k}^{\ast}|+2|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-\lambda^{S}|G_{k}^{\ast}|-|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}=0.

This concludes the verification of condition (C3).

2. Conditions (C1) and (C5).

We now show that the condition
\lambda^{S}<U^{S}(\mathbf{X})
ensures conditions (C1) and (C5). Since our construction sets the diagonal block
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}
as a zero matrix, it suffices to show that if
\lambda^{S}<U^{S}(\mathbf{X})
, then

\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}>0

for all
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
.
By our construction in (
17
),
for
i\in G_{k}^{\ast}
and
j\in G_{l}^{\ast}
,
the
(i,j)
th element of
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}
is

\frac{r_{i}^{(k,l,S)}\,r_{j}^{(l,k,S)}}{\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}r_{j}^{(l,k,S)}.}

(41)

where

r_{i}^{(k,l,S)}=-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\lambda^{S}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr),

and

r_{j}^{(l,k,S)}=-\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|+|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\lambda^{S}+\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}-\mathbf{X}_{S,j}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}-\mathbf{X}_{S,j}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr).

Therefore, all elements of
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}
for all
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
are strictly positive if,
for all
1\leq k\neq l\leq K

and
i\in G_{k}^{\ast}
,
the inequality

r_{i}^{(k,l,S)}>0
holds, or equivalently,

\lambda^{S}<\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}||G_{k}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,l}^{\ast}-\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}-\mathbf{X}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr).

This condition is precisely
\lambda^{S}<U^{S}(\mathbf{X})
, completing the proof of Lemma
16
.
∎

C.4.4
Proof of Lemma
17

The proof proceeds in four steps:

1.

Reduction to uniform control of a quadratic form over a subspace (equation (
42
)),

2.

Derivation of
L^{S}_{1}(\mathbf{X})
(equation (
44
)),

3.

Derivation of
t_{lk}^{S}(\lambda^{S})
(equation (
45
)),

4.

Derivation of
T^{S}_{1,kl}
,
T^{S}_{2,kl}
, and
T^{S}_{3,kl}
(equation
46
).

Proof.

Given
\lambda_{S}
, we construct
\dot{\mathbf{B}}(\lambda_{S})
,
\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}(\lambda_{S})
, and
\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}(\lambda_{S})
according to Lemma
15
and denote them by
\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}
,
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}
, and
\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}
, respectively,
for notational simplicity.

1. Reduction to uniform control of a quadratic form over a subspace.

By Lemma
16
, these constructed dual variables satisfy condition (C4) of Lemma
13
. Therefore,
by Remark
28
, for any
k=1,\ldots,K
, we have

\bigl(\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigr)_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{0},
from (
14
), and for all
l\neq k
, we have

(\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}})_{G_{l}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{0}
by (
15
).
Therefore, we have

\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{0}
for
k=1,\ldots,K
.
This implies that
\mathbf{1}_{G_{1}^{\ast}},\ldots,\mathbf{1}_{G_{K}^{\ast}}
are eigenvectors of
\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}
corresponding to zero eigenvalues.
Since any vector in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
can be decomposed into the sum of its projections onto

\operatorname{span}\left(\mathbf{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}:k\in[K]\right)

and its orthogonal complement, denoted as
\Gamma_{K}
,
to show that
\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\succeq 0
, it suffices to prove that

\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{v}\geq 0\quad\text{for all }\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K}\text{ such that }\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1.

(42)

2. Derivation of
L^{S}_{1}(\mathbf{X})
.

By the definition of
\Gamma_{k}
,
\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{k}
implies

\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{G_{1}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{G_{2}^{\ast}}=\ldots=\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{G_{K}^{\ast}}=0,

which implies

\sum_{j\in G_{k}^{\ast}}^{n}v_{j}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}=0~\text{for all}~k=1,\ldots,K.

(43)

Now we compute the quadratic form
\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{v}

for

\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K}
such that
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\lambda^{S}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\textbf{I}_{n}\mathbf{v}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{n}(\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S})^{\top}\mathbf{v}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\lambda^{S}+(\mathbf{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{S})\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}-\|\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\lambda^{S}-\|\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}-\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}\mathbf{v},

where
step
(i)
uses the construction

\dot{\mathbf{W}}^{S}=\lambda^{S}\textbf{I}_{n}+\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{1}_{n}(\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S})^{\top}+\frac{1}{2}\dot{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{S}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}-\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}-\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}
,
step
(ii)

uses

\mathbf{v}^{\top}\textbf{I}_{n}\mathbf{v}=\|\mathbf{v}\|^{2}=1
,
and
step
(ii)

uses (
43
).
Using our model and notation in Definition
12
,

\mathbf{X}_{S,\cdot}\mathbf{v}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\mathbf{X}_{S,i}=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\biggl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}v_{i}(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k})_{S}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\biggr)\overset{(i)}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\mathcal{E}_{S,i}=\mathcal{E}_{S,\cdot}\mathbf{v},

where step
(i)
uses (
43
).
Therefore, a sufficient condition for (
42
) is:

\lambda^{S}\geq\underbrace{\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\hskip-13.00005pt\|\mathcal{E}_{S,\cdot}\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}}_{L^{S}_{1}(\mathbf{X})}+\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\hskip-13.00005pt\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}\mathbf{v}.

(44)

This completes the derivation of
L^{S}_{1}(\mathbf{X})
.

3. Derivation of
t_{lk}^{S}(\lambda^{S})
.

Now we start to derive
L_{2}^{S}(\mathbf{X},\lambda^{S})
.
Recall from
Lemma
15
that
given
\lambda^{S}
,
we construct
\dot{\mathbf{B}}
as a block-diagonal matrix, where the diagonal blocks are zero matrices and
for
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
, the
(k,l)
th off-diagonal block is defined as:

\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}=\frac{1}{\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{(l,k,S)}}\,\mathbf{r}^{(k,l,S)}\,(\mathbf{r}^{(l,k,S)})^{\top},

with
\mathbf{r}^{(k,l,S)}\in\mathbb{R}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}
defined componentwise by

r_{i}^{(k,l,S)}=-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\lambda^{S}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr).

Using this definition, we compute

\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}\mathbf{v}
in (
44
):

\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}\mathbf{v}\overset{(i)}{=}\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\mathbf{v}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}\mathbf{v}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}\overset{(ii)}{=}\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\frac{(\mathbf{v}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{(k,l,S)})(\mathbf{v}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{(l,k,S)})}{\underbrace{\mathbf{1}_{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{(l,k,S)}}_{=t_{lk}^{S}(\lambda^{S})}},

(45)

where
step
(i)
uses

\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{k}^{\ast}}=\mathbf{0}
for
k=1,\ldots,K
,
and
step
(ii)
uses
our construction of
\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}_{G_{k}^{\ast},G_{l}^{\ast}}
.

4. Derivation of
T^{S}_{1,kl}
,
T^{S}_{2,kl}
, and
T^{S}_{3,kl}
.

From (
45
), we analyze one term in the numerator:

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{(k,l,S)}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}r_{i}^{(k,l,S)}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\left\{-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\lambda^{S}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}-\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\bigl(\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l},\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\rangle-\|\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}+2\langle\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k},\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\rangle\bigr)

\displaystyle\quad\overset{(ii)}{=}|G_{l}^{\ast}|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\langle\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,k}-\overline{\mathbf{X}}^{\ast}_{S,l},\;\mathbf{X}_{S,i}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\overset{(iii)}{=}|G_{l}^{\ast}|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S}+\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k}^{\ast}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\;{\mathcal{E}}_{S,i}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=|G_{l}^{\ast}|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\;{\mathcal{E}}_{S\cap S_{0},i}\rangle+|G_{l}^{\ast}|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\langle\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k}^{\ast}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\;{\mathcal{E}}_{S,i}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=I^{S}_{kl}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})+N_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})

where step
(i)
and
(ii)
use
\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}=0
for
k=1,\ldots,K
,
and step
(iii)
comes from our model and notation in Definition
12
.
Similarly, we have

\mathbf{v}_{G_{l}^{\ast}}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{(l,k,S)}=I^{S}_{lk}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})+N_{lk}^{S}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X}).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}\dot{\mathbf{B}}^{S}\mathbf{v}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\frac{1}{t_{lk}^{S}(\lambda^{S})}\bigl(I^{S}_{kl}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})+N_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})\bigr)\bigl(I^{S}_{lk}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})+N_{lk}^{S}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\frac{1}{t_{lk}^{S}(\lambda^{S})}\bigl(T^{S}_{1,kl}(\mathbf{v})+T^{S}_{2,kl}(\mathbf{v})+T^{S}_{3,kl}(\mathbf{v})\bigr).

(46)

Plugging this into (
44
) completes the proof of Lemma
17
.

∎

C.4.5
Proof of Lemma
19

For notational simplicity, we assume
\sigma^{2}=1
here.
The proof proceeds in the following steps:

1.

Decomposition of
D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})
into Chi-squares, Gaussian and inner product of Gaussians,

2.

Bounding the Gaussian and Chi-squares using the tail bounds,

3.

Bounding the Gaussian inner product using conditioning, Gaussian tail bounds and separation condition,

4.

Defining the uniform bound event,

5.

Bounding the probability of the uniform bound event.

Proof.

We proceed by following the steps outlined above in sequence.

1. Decomposition of
D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})
into Chi-squares, Gaussian and inner product of Gaussians.

Fix indices
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
and choose
i\in G_{k}^{\ast}
.
Based on our model and notation in Definition
12
,
we consider the following decomposition of
D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})
:

\displaystyle D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})~=~

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{X}_{S,i}-\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\mathbf{X}_{S,i}-\overline{\mathbf{X}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle~=~

\displaystyle\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S}+\mathcal{E}_{S,i}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle~=~

\displaystyle\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S}+\mathcal{E}_{S,i}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-\left(\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|-1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\right)^{2}\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}~

\displaystyle\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S},\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-2\left\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S\cap S_{0},l}^{\ast}\right\rangle-2\left\langle\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|-1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\right)^{2}\left(\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-2\left\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}\right\rangle\right)

\displaystyle~=~

\displaystyle\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2|G_{k}^{\ast}|-1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}}\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle-\left(\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|-1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\right)^{2}\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}-2\left\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S\cap S_{0},l}^{\ast}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+2\left\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}+\left(\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|-1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\right)^{2}\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast},\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\right\rangle,

(47)

where step
(i)
uses the sparsity assumption on cluster center difference.

The six terms in this decomposition are classified into the following categories:

•

Signal:
\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{l})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}

•

Chi-square noise:

\displaystyle\operatorname{CN}_{l}^{S}

\displaystyle:=\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\operatorname{CN}_{i}^{S}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2|G^{\ast}_{k}|-1}{|G^{\ast}_{k}|^{2}}\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2},~\text{and}

\displaystyle\operatorname{CN}_{ki}^{S}

\displaystyle:=-\left(\frac{|G^{\ast}_{k}|-1}{|G^{\ast}_{k}|}\right)^{2}\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}\|_{2}^{2}

•

Gaussian signal-noise interaction:

\operatorname{GSN}_{kl}^{S}:=-2\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{l})_{S\cap S_{0}},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S\cap S_{0},l}^{\ast}\rangle

•

Inner product of Gaussians:

\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S}:=2\left\langle\bigl(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}\bigr)_{S}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}+\left(\frac{|G^{\ast}_{k}|-1}{|G^{\ast}_{k}|}\right)^{2}\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast},\;\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\right\rangle,

In summary, we have:

D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})~=~\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{l})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+\operatorname{CN}_{l}^{S}+\operatorname{CN}_{i}^{S}+\operatorname{CN}_{ki}^{S}+\operatorname{GSN}_{kl}^{S}+\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S}.

(48)

2. Bounding the Gaussian and Chi-squares using the tail inequalities.

Chi-square noise and Gaussian signal-noise interaction terms

(\operatorname{CN}_{l}^{S},\operatorname{CN}_{i}^{S},\operatorname{CN}_{ki}^{S}
, and
\operatorname{GSN}_{kl}^{S})

in (
48
) are straightforward to bound from below. Define the following lower bound events:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(i,1)}_{kl,S}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\operatorname{CN}_{l}^{S}\geq\frac{|S|-2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\right\},

(49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(i,2)}_{kl,S}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\operatorname{CN}_{i}^{S}\geq\frac{2|G^{\ast}_{k}|-1}{|G^{\ast}_{k}|^{2}}\bigl(|S|-2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}\bigr)\right\},

(50)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(i,3)}_{kl,S}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\operatorname{CN}_{ki}^{S}\geq\frac{1-|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}}\bigl(|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}+2\zeta^{S}\bigr)\right\},

(51)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(i,4)}_{kl,S}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\operatorname{GSN}_{kl}^{S}\geq-2\sqrt{\frac{2\zeta^{S}}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}\right\},

(52)

By setting
\zeta^{S}=\log(n^{2}|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
,
Lemma
3
implies that each event has a probability of at least

1-1/(n^{2}K^{2}|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
, for any
S\in\mathcal{S}
.

3. Bounding the Gaussian inner product using conditioning, Gaussian tail bounds and separation condition.

Here, we use the separation condition to bound the term.
If we condition on
\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}
and
\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}
,
then
-\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S}
has a univariate Gaussian distribution with mean zero and variance as the following:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(-\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S}\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}+(1-|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})^{2}\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\underbrace{\left\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}-(1-|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})^{2}\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}\right\|_{2}^{2}}_{:=\operatorname{CN}_{kli}^{S}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{2\bigl\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\;(1-|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})^{2}\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S\cap S_{0},k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}-\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S\cap S_{0},l}^{\ast}\bigr\rangle}_{:=\operatorname{GN}_{kli}^{S}},

where step
(i)
uses our sparsity assumption on cluster center difference.

Next, we define events that bound this variance from above:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(i,5)}_{kl,S}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\operatorname{CN}_{kli}^{S}\leq(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})(|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}+2\zeta^{S})\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{(i,6)}_{kl,S}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\operatorname{GN}_{kli}^{S}\leq 2\sqrt{2(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})\zeta^{S}}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}\right\}.

By setting
\zeta^{S}=\log(n^{2}|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
,
Lemma
3
implies that each event has a probability of at least

1-1/(n^{2}|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
, uniformly overall all
S\in\mathcal{S}
.

Thus, conditioned on events
\mathcal{B}^{(i,5)}_{kl,S}
and

\mathcal{B}^{(i,6)}_{kl,S}
, the variance can be bouneded as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(-\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S}\;|\ \;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast})

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})(|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}+2\zeta^{S})

\displaystyle\quad+2\sigma^{2}\sqrt{2(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})\zeta^{S}}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}.

To further bound this variance,
it is convenient to remove the first-order signal term
\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}
. To this end,
we collect some conditions.
First, recall from Theorem
1
that

m=2\min_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\bigl(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1}\bigr)^{-1}.

From (
6
), recall that for

S\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

\min_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\sigma^{2}\left(\log n+\frac{|S|\log p}{m}+\sqrt{\frac{|S|\log p}{m}}\right),

(53)

and
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
.
Under these conditions, by treating
K
as a costant,
we have

\zeta^{S}=\log(n^{2}|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})\lesssim|S|\log p+\log n,

which implies

2\sqrt{2(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})\zeta^{S}}\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\zeta^{S}}{m}}\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Therefore, we can further bound the variance as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}(-\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S})

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})(|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}+2\zeta^{S}).

Therefore, by the Gaussian tail inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{CP}_{kli}^{S}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}\left(-\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S}>\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|^{2}_{2}\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(\frac{-\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{4}}{2\mathrm{Var}(-\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S}\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}^{\ast})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\left(\frac{-\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{4}}{\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2}+2(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})(|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}+2\zeta^{S})}\right),

(54)

where the last step uses the variance bound.
We want to bound the last term by
C/n^{2}
, with
C
a constant.
To this end, it suffices to show

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{4}}{\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|^{2}+2(|G_{l}^{\ast}|^{-1}+|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{-1})(|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}+2\zeta^{S})}\gtrsim\log n,

which is indeed satisfied under our separation condition in (
53
).

4. Defining the uniform bound event.

We collect the bounds in
(
49
),
(
50
),
(
51
),
(
52
), and
(
54
).
From (
48
), recalled below,

D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})~=~\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{l})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+\operatorname{CN}_{l}^{S}+\operatorname{CN}_{i}^{S}+\operatorname{CN}_{ki}^{S}+\operatorname{GSN}_{kl}^{S}+\mathrm{IP}_{kli}^{S}.

we can say

\displaystyle D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})~\gtrsim

\displaystyle~\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{|S|-2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}+\frac{2|G^{\ast}_{k}|-1}{|G^{\ast}_{k}|^{2}}\bigl(|S|-2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}\bigr)

\displaystyle~+~\frac{1-|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}}\bigl(|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}+2\zeta^{S}\bigr)-2\sqrt{\frac{2\zeta^{S}}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle~+~\left\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle~\gtrsim

\displaystyle~\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}-\sqrt{\frac{\zeta^{S}}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle~-~\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)|S|-\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}-\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\zeta^{S}

\displaystyle~=

\displaystyle~\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}~-~\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)|S|-R^{S}_{kl},

where

R^{S}_{kl}=\sqrt{\frac{\zeta^{S}}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}+\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)\sqrt{|S|\zeta^{S}}+\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\zeta^{S}.

As a result, we define an event

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kli}:=\Bigg\{D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})\gtrsim\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)|S|-R^{S}_{kl}\Bigg\},

(55)

with the index
(k,l,i)
ranging
1\leq k\neq l\leq K

and all
i\in G^{*}_{k}
, and
S
ranging over
\mathcal{S}
.
The proof is complete once we show that the intersection event

\bigcap_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\bigcap_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigcap_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kli}

holds with high probability.

5. Bounding the probability of the uniform bound event.

To prove the lemma, it suffices to show that

\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\bigcup_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}{\bigl(\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kli}\bigr)}^{C}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{n}.

To show this, first denote

\mathcal{B}^{(i)}_{kl,S}:=\bigcap_{\ell=1}^{6}\mathcal{B}^{(i,\ell)}_{kl,S}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl(\bigcup_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}

\displaystyle\bigcup_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}{(\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kli})^{C}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{P}{\bigl((\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kli})^{C}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kli})^{C}\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kli})^{C}\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,k\setminus\{i\}}\bigr)\mathbbm{1}(\mathcal{B}^{(i)}_{kl,S})+\mathbbm{1}(\mathcal{B}^{(i)}_{kl,S})^{C}\bigr]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\left(\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{S\in\mathcal{S},|S|=q}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathrm{CP}_{kli}^{S}\mathbbm{1}(\mathcal{B}^{(i)}_{kl,S})\bigr]\right)+\frac{\pi^{2}}{n}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}\frac{K^{2}\pi^{2}}{6n}+\frac{K^{2}\pi^{2}}{n},

where
step
(i)
uses

\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}(1/q^{2})=\pi^{2}/6
, and
step
(ii)
uses (
54
).
Treating
K
as a constant, we conclude that

D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})\gtrsim\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)|S|-R^{S}_{kl}.

uniformly over
S\in\mathcal{S}
, with probability at least
1-C/n
, where
C>0
is a constant.
This completes the proof of Lemma
19
.

∎

C.4.6
Proof of Lemma
20

Proof.

Given a support set
S\subset[p]
with
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
,
we have

\displaystyle L_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{X})=\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\hskip-13.00005pt\|\mathcal{E}_{S,\cdot}\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\|\mathcal{E}_{S,\cdot}\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\mathcal{E}_{S,\cdot}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}.

Here,

\mathcal{E}_{S,\cdot}
is a
|S|\times n
random matrix with i.i.d. Gaussian entries distributed as
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
.
Therefore,
by Lemma
7
,
if we define an event

\mathcal{C}_{L}^{S}:=\bigl\{L_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{X})\leq\sigma^{2}(n+|S|+\xi^{S})\bigr\},

(56)

where
\xi^{S}=\log(n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
,
then we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{C}_{L}^{S})\geq 1-1/(n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}}).

Now we show that the event
\mathcal{C}_{L}^{S}
holds uniformly across all
S\subset[p]
such that
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
, with probability at least
1-C_{8}/n
, where
C_{8}>0
is a constant.

\mathbb{P}\biggl(\bigcup_{S\subset[p],|S|\leq\sqrt{p}}(\mathcal{C}_{L}^{S})^{C}\biggr)\leq\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\mathbb{P}((\mathcal{C}_{L}^{S})^{C})\leq\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\frac{1}{n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}}}=\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\frac{1}{nq^{2}}\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{6n},

where the last
step uses

\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}=\pi^{2}/6.

Since
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
,
|S|\log p
dominates
\log|S|
. Therefore, we conclude that
uniformly across
S\in[p]
such that
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
,
with probability at least
1-C_{8}/n
, it holds that

L_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{X})\lesssim\sigma^{2}(n+\log n+|S|\log p).

This completes the proof of Lemma
20
.
∎

C.4.7
Proof of Lemma
21

The proof proceeds in the following steps:

1.

The first inequality

L_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{X}).\lesssim\dot{\lambda}^{S}
.

2.

The second inequality

\dot{\lambda}^{S}\lesssim U^{S}(\mathbf{X})
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we assume
\sigma^{2}=1
here.

1. The first inequality.

We aim to show

L_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{X})\lesssim\dot{\lambda}^{S}
.
From (
6
), recall that for

S\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}=\min_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}\gtrsim\sigma^{2}\left(\log n+\frac{|S|\log p}{m}+\sqrt{\frac{|S|\log p}{m}}\right),

(57)

where

m=2\min_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\left\{\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)^{-1}\right\}.

Therefore,
with probability at least
1-C_{8}/n
uniformly across all
S\in\mathcal{S}
, where
C_{8}>0
is a constant from Lemma
20
, it holds that

\displaystyle L_{1}^{S}(\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle\lesssim(n+\log n+|S|\log p)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}|S|+\frac{1}{4}\bigl(m\log n+|S|\log p+\sqrt{m|S|\log p}\bigr)

\displaystyle\lesssim|S|+\frac{m}{4}\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\dot{\lambda}^{S},

where step
(i)
uses Lemma
20
.
This completes the derivation of the first inequality.

2. The second inequality.

We aim to show

\dot{\lambda}^{S}\lesssim U^{S}(\mathbf{X})
.
Recall from
(
19
) that

U^{S}(\mathbf{X})=\min_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)^{-1}\min_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}D^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})\biggr\}.

By Lemma
19
, with probability at least

1-C_{7}/n
uniformly across
S\in\mathcal{S}
and
uniformly over
(k,l,i)
where
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
and
i\in G_{k}^{\ast}
, we have

\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)^{-1}\hskip-4.49997ptD^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})\gtrsim\underbrace{\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)^{-1}\hskip-4.49997pt\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+|S|}_{\text{Part A}}\underbrace{-\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)^{-1}\hskip-4.49997ptR^{S}_{kl}}_{\text{Part B}},

where

R^{S}_{kl}=\sqrt{\frac{\xi^{S}}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}+\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)\sqrt{|S|\xi^{S}}+\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\xi^{S},

and
\xi^{S}=\log(n^{2}K^{2}|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
.
Under the separation condition for
\mathcal{S}
, recalled in (
57
), and noting that
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
for all
S\in\mathcal{S}
, Part A dominates Part B in the inequality above. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\min_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\left\{\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)^{-1}\hskip-4.49997ptD^{S}_{kli}(\mathbf{X})\right\}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\min_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\biggl(\frac{1}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}+\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)^{-1}\hskip-4.49997pt\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+|S|

\displaystyle\gtrsim|S|+\frac{m}{4}\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\dot{\lambda}^{S}.

This concludes the derivation of the second inequality and thereby completes the proof.
∎

C.4.8
Proof of Lemma
22

Proof.

For notational simplicity, let
\sigma^{2}=1
.
Fix
S\in\mathcal{S}
and
(l,k)
with
1\leq l\neq k\leq K
. Condition on the high-probability event in Lemma
19
. Then we have

\displaystyle t^{S}_{lk}(\dot{\lambda}^{S})

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\biggl\{\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}D_{lkj}(\mathbf{X},S)-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\dot{\lambda}^{S}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\biggl\{\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}D_{lkj}(\mathbf{X},S)-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\bigl(|S|+\frac{m}{4}\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}\bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\gtrsim}\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\biggl\{\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}\bigl(|S|+\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\bigl(|S|+\frac{m}{4}\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}\bigr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\geq}\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\biggl(\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}|S|+\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.00006pt-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{l}^{\ast}|}|S|-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\frac{m}{4}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}\biggr)

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\geq}\sum_{j\in G_{l}^{\ast}}\biggl\{\biggl(\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\biggr)|S|+\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}{4}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}||G_{k}^{\ast}|}{4}\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}||G_{k}^{\ast}|}{2}|S|-\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{2}|S|.

\displaystyle\overset{(v)}{\gtrsim}|G_{l}^{\ast}||G_{k}^{\ast}|\bigl(\|\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}+|S|\bigr)

where
step
(i)
follows from the definition of
t^{S}_{lk}(\lambda^{S})
in (
26
),
step
(ii)
follows from the definition of
\dot{\lambda}_{S}
in Lemma
21
,
step
(iii)
uses

-\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}>-\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}
,
step
(iv)
uses

-m\geq-2\left\{\frac{|G_{k}^{\ast}|\cdot|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\right\},

and step
(v)
uses
|G_{k}^{\ast}|\geq 2
and
|G_{l}^{\ast}|\geq 2
.
This completes the proof of Lemma
22
.
∎

C.4.9
Proof of Lemma
23

the proof proceeds in the following steps.

1.

Individual bound by Chi-square tail bound,

2.

Uniform bound.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, let
\sigma^{2}=1
.

1. Individual bound by Chi-square tail bound.

Fix
(k,l)
such that
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
and

S\in\mathcal{S}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}I^{S}_{kl}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}|G_{l}^{\ast}|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\;\mathcal{E}_{S\cap S_{0},i}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}|G_{l}^{\ast}|\biggl|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\;\mathcal{E}_{S\cap S_{0},i}\rangle\biggr|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}|G_{l}^{\ast}|\biggl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}\biggl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigl\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\mathcal{E}_{S\cap S_{0},i}\bigr\rangle^{2}\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq|G_{l}^{\ast}|\biggl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigl\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\mathcal{E}_{S\cap S_{0},i}\bigr\rangle^{2}\biggr)^{1/2},

where step
(i)
uses the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

Now we derive an inequality for
\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigl\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\mathcal{E}_{S\cap S_{0},i}\bigr\rangle^{2}
.
Since

\frac{1}{\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}}\bigl\langle(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}},\mathcal{E}_{S\cap S_{0},i}\bigr\rangle\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,1),\quad i=1,\ldots,|G_{k}^{\ast}|,

the square of this quantity follows a chi-square distribution with
|G_{k}^{\ast}|
degrees of freedom, provided that
S
is nonempty. Applying the chi-square tail inequality (Lemma
3
), if we define

\mathcal{C}_{Ikl}^{S}:=\biggl\{\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}I^{S}_{kl}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})\leq\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}\bigl(|G_{k}^{\ast}|+2\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|\zeta_{I}^{S}}+2\zeta_{I}^{S}\bigr)^{1/2}\biggr\},

(58)

where

\zeta_{I}^{S}=\log(nK^{2}|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
,
then we have

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{C}_{Ikl}^{S}\bigr)\geq 1-1/(n^{2}K^{2}|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
.

2. Uniform bound.

Now we show that the event
\mathcal{C}_{Ikl}^{S}
holds uniformly across all
S\subset\mathcal{S}
and
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
, with probability at least
1-C_{10}/n
, where
C_{10}>0
is a constant.

\mathbb{P}\bigl(\bigcup_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{S}}(\mathcal{C}_{Ikl}^{S})^{C}\bigr)\leq\hskip-3.99994pt\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\mathbb{P}((\mathcal{C}_{Ikl}^{S})^{C})\overset{(i)}{\leq}\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\frac{1}{n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}}}=\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\frac{1}{nq^{2}}\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{6n},

where the last
step uses

\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}=\pi^{2}/6.

Since
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
,
|S|\log p
dominates
\log|S|
. Therefore, we conclude that
uniformly across
S\in\mathcal{S}
and
(k,l)
such that
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
,
with probability at least
1-C_{10}/n
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}I^{S}_{kl}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\lesssim|G_{l}^{\ast}|\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}\biggl(|G_{k}^{\ast}|+\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|\log n}+\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}||S|\log p}+\log n+|S|\log p\biggr)^{1/2}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
23
.
∎

C.4.10
Proof of Lemma
24

Proof.

For notational simplicity,
let
\sigma^{2}=1
and
for
k=1,\ldots,K
, denote

\mathrm{IB}_{k}:=\biggl(|G_{k}^{\ast}|+\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|\log n}+\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}||S|\log p}+\log n+|S|\log p\biggr)^{1/2}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\frac{T^{S}_{1,kl}(\mathbf{v})}{t^{S}_{lk}(\dot{\lambda}^{S})}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\frac{I^{S}_{kl}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})\times I^{S}_{lk}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|\cdot|G_{k}^{\ast}|\cdot\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{l})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}+|S|}

\displaystyle{\leq}\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\frac{I^{S}_{kl}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})\times I^{S}_{lk}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|\cdot|G_{k}^{\ast}|\cdot\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{k}-\boldsymbol{\mu}^{\ast}_{l})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\mathrm{IB_{k}^{S}}\times\mathrm{IB_{l}^{S}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{IB_{k}^{S}}\;\sum_{l=1}^{K}\mathrm{IB_{l}^{S}}

\displaystyle\overset{(iii)}{\lesssim}n+\sqrt{n\log n}+\sqrt{n|S|\log p}+\log n+|S|\log p

\displaystyle\overset{(iv)}{\lesssim}|S|+m\log n+\sqrt{m|S|\log p}+|S|\log p

\displaystyle\overset{(v)}{\lesssim}|S|+\frac{m}{4}\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}=\dot{\lambda}^{S},

where step
(i)
uses Lemma
22

and
definition

T^{S}_{1,kl}(\mathbf{v})=I^{S}_{kl}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})\times I^{S}_{lk}(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{X})

presented in (
23
),
step
(ii)
uses Lemma

23
,
step
(iii)
uses the identity

\sum_{k=1}^{K}|G_{k}^{\ast}|=n

and
inequality
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\leq\sqrt{K\sum_{k=1}^{K}|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\lesssim\sqrt{n}
,
step
(iv)
uses the condition

m\gtrsim n/\log n
of Theorem
1
,
and step
(v)
uses the separation condition of
\mathcal{S}
, presented in (
6
).
This completes the proof of Lemma
24
.
∎

C.4.11
Proof of Lemma
25

With appropriate algebraic manipulation, the problem reduces to an application of the concentration inequality for an unbounded empirical process developed by
Adamczak, 2008
(Lemma
8
). This requires estimating the expectation, variance, and sub-exponential norm of the empirical process. Accordingly, the proof proceeds in the following steps.

1.

Reformulation into a quadratic form,

2.

Bounding the operator norm dependent terms,

3.

Bounding the expectation of the supremum,

4.

Individual bound using Hanson-wright inequality,

5.

Uniform bound.

Proof.

For notational simplicity, we set
\sigma^{2}=1
and assume, without loss of generality, that
G_{k}^{\ast}={1,\dots,|G_{k}^{\ast}|}
throughout the proof.

1. Transformation into a quadratic form.

Fix
(k,l)
such that
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
and
S\subset[p]
such that
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
.
We can express
G^{S}_{3,kl}(\mathbf{v})
as a scaled quadratic form:

\displaystyle G^{S}_{1,kl}(\mathbf{v})

\displaystyle=\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}\}}\hskip-10.00002ptv_{i}\left\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\mathcal{E}_{S,j}\right\rangle

(59)

\displaystyle=\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}\}}\hskip-10.00002pt\frac{1}{2}(v_{i}+v_{j})\left\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\mathcal{E}_{S,j}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\underbrace{\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})^{\top}(\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{|S|})\;\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})}_{\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{|S|},\mathbf{X})},

Here,
the
(i,j)
th element of the matrix

\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|\times|G_{k}^{\ast}|}

is defined as
A^{\mathbf{v}}_{i,j}:=(v_{i}+v_{j})/2
, and

\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})
denotes the
|S|\cdot|G_{k}^{\ast}|
-dimensional vector obtained by stacking the columns of
\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}}
in order, with the first column at the top and the last column at the bottom,
and
\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{|S|}
denotes the tensor product:

\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{|S|}=\begin{bmatrix}A^{\mathbf{v}}_{1,1}\mathbf{I}_{|S|}&\cdots&A^{\mathbf{v}}_{1,|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\mathbf{I}_{|S|}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
A^{\mathbf{v}}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|,1}\mathbf{I}_{|S|}&\cdots&A^{\mathbf{v}}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|,|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\mathbf{I}_{|S|}\end{bmatrix}.

The quadratic form

\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{|S|},\mathbf{X})

is centered because

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{|S|},\mathbf{X})\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}\}}\hskip-10.00002ptv_{i}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\left\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\mathcal{E}_{S,j}\right\rangle\bigr]=\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]\overset{(i)}{=}|S|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}=0,

where step
(i)
uses
\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}=0
, shown in (
43
),
and
indexed by

\mathcal{M}^{S}_{kl}=\{\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{|S|}\mid\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1\}.

To derive the uniform bound, we apply the uniform Hanson-Wright inequality (Lemma
11
) to obtain the concentration inequality for

\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{M}^{S}_{kl}}\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{M},\mathbf{X})-\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{M}^{S}_{kl}}\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{M},\mathbf{X})\biggr].

To apply Lemma
11
,
we need to bound the following two quantities:

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\quad\text{and}\quad\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|_{\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}\|(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{M}^{\top})\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|_{2}\bigr],

where bounding both terms critically depends on
\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
,
and the expectation of supremum

\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{M}^{S}_{kl}}\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{M},\mathbf{X})\biggr].

We present the bounds in order.

2. Bounding the operator norm dependent terms.

We start with
\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
.
First note that for
\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}
, its operator norm can be simplified into:

\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{|S|}\|_{\text{op}}=\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\text{op}}\|\mathbf{I}_{|S|}\|_{\text{op}}=\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\text{op}}.

Therefore it suffices to bound the supremum of
\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\text{op}}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\|_{\text{op}}

\displaystyle=\max_{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}=1,\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}}\mathbf{u}^{T}\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}}\mathbf{u}

\displaystyle=\max_{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}=1,\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}}\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}\}}\hskip-10.00002ptu_{i}u_{j}\frac{v_{i}+v_{j}}{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}=1,\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}}\bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}u_{i}v_{i}\bigr)\bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}u_{j}\bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\max_{\|\mathbf{u}\|_{2}=1,\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}}\bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}u_{i}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\bigl(\sum_{i=1}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}v_{i}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}u_{j}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|},

where step
(i)
uses Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
and
step
(ii)
uses
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\leq 1
for
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}
.
Since the last term do not depend on
\mathbf{v}
, we have

\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}.

(60)

Next, we bound
\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|^{2}_{\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|_{\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}\|(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{M}^{\top})\;\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|_{2}\bigr]\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bigl(\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}\|(\mathbf{M}+\mathbf{M}^{\top})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\;\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|_{2}\bigr]\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\bigl(\mathbb{E}\bigl[2|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{1/2}\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|_{2}\bigr]\bigr)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}4|G_{k}^{\ast}|\;\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=4|G_{k}^{\ast}|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr]

\displaystyle=4|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}|S|,

where step
(i)
uses (
60
),
step
(ii)
applies Jensen’s inequality to a convex function
x\mapsto x^{2}
.

3. Bounding the expectation of the supremum.

It is more convenient to start from (
59
) and decompose the quadratic form
\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{X})
into the following two terms:

\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{A}^{\mathbf{v}},\mathbf{X})=\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}:i\neq j\}}\hskip-10.00002ptv_{j}\bigl\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\mathcal{E}_{S,j}\bigr\rangle+\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}v_{i}\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}.

For these two terms, the index set
\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}
, which indexes the quadratic form, is more suitably represented by

\mathbb{V}_{k}:=\left\{\mathbf{v}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}:\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1\right\}.

We provide the bound for each of these two terms, starting with the first one.
First note that given
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}:i\neq j\}}\hskip-10.00002ptv_{j}\bigl\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\mathcal{E}_{S,j}\bigr\rangle

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\bigl|\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}:i\neq j\}}\hskip-10.00002ptv_{j}\bigl\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\mathcal{E}_{S,j}\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\bigl|\sum_{j\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{j}\bigl\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,j},\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast},i\neq j}\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\bigr\rangle\bigr|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\bigl(\sum_{j\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{j}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\bigl(\sum_{j\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigl\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,j},\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast},i\neq j}\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\bigr\rangle^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\bigl(\sum_{j\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigl\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,j},\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast},i\neq j}\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\bigr\rangle^{2}\bigr)^{1/2},

where
step
(i)
uses Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
and
step
(ii)
uses
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\leq 1
for
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}
.
Using this bound, we can upper bound the expectation as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}:i\neq j\}}\hskip-10.00002ptv_{j}\bigl\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\mathcal{E}_{S,j}\bigr\rangle\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\biggl[\bigl(\sum_{j\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\bigl\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,j},\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast},i\neq j}\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\bigr\rangle^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\biggl(\sum_{j\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\bigl\langle(\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}_{j})_{S},\sum_{i\neq j,i\in[n_{k}]}\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\bigr\rangle^{2}\bigr]\biggr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\bigl(\sum_{j\in G_{k}^{\ast}}\mathbb{E}\|\hskip-6.99997pt\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast},i\neq j}\hskip-6.99997pt\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\;\bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\bigl(|G_{k}^{\ast}|(|G_{k}^{\ast}|-1)|S|\bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq|G_{k}^{\ast}|\sqrt{|S|},

(61)

where step
(i)
uses Jensen’s inequality to a concave function
x\mapsto x^{1/2}
,
step
(ii)
the independence and zero-mean assumption,
and step
(iii)
uses isotropic Gaussian assumption.

Next, we bound the expectation of the supremum of
\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}v_{i}\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\bigl|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}v_{i}\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\bigl|\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}v_{i}\bigr(\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-|S|\bigl)\bigr|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}v_{i}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\biggl\{\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}\left(\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-|S|\right)^{2}\biggr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}\left(\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-|S|\right)^{2}\biggr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{\leq}}\biggl\{\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}\mathbb{E}\left(\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}-|S|\right)^{2}\biggr\}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}\mathrm{Var}\|\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\|_{2}^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\overset{(v)}{=}\sqrt{2|G_{k}^{\ast}||S|},

(62)

where step
(i)
uses
\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}=0
, which follows from the fact that
\Gamma_{k}
is orthogonal to
\mathbbm{1}_{G_{k}^{\ast}}
,
step
(ii)
uses Cauchy-Schwarz inequality,
step
(iii)
uses
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}\leq 1
,
step
(iv)
uses Jensen’s inequality to a concave function
x\mapsto x^{1/2}
,
and step
(v)
uses our Gaussian noise assumption and the variance formula for Chi-square random variables.

Combining (
61
) and (
62
), we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\biggl[\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{M}^{S}_{kl}}\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{M},\mathbf{X})\biggr]\lesssim|G_{k}^{\ast}|\sqrt{|S|}.

4. Individual bound using Hanson-wright inequality.

Applying Lemma
11
with the bounds established in the previous steps and using the formulation (
59
) of
G^{S}_{1,kl}(\mathbf{v})
, we define an event

\mathcal{C}_{N,kl}^{S}:=\biggl\{\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{V}_{k}}\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\sum_{\{(i,j)\in G_{k}^{\ast}\}}\hskip-10.00002ptv_{i}\left\langle\mathcal{E}_{S,i},\mathcal{E}_{S,j}\right\rangle\lesssim\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\underbrace{|G_{k}^{\ast}|\sqrt{|S|}}_{\hskip-81.94473pt\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{M}^{S}_{kl}}\mathrm{QF}_{kl}^{S}(\mathbf{M},\mathbf{X})\bigr]\lesssim}~+~\frac{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}\zeta^{S}_{N,kl}\biggr\},

where
\zeta^{S}_{N,kl}:=|G_{k}^{\ast}|\sqrt{|S|(\log n+2\log|S|+\log{{\binom{p}{|S|}}})}
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl((\mathcal{C}_{N,kl}^{S})^{C}\biggr)

\displaystyle\lesssim\exp\biggl[-\min\biggl(\underbrace{\frac{(\zeta^{S}_{N,kl})^{2}}{|G_{k}^{\ast}|^{2}|S|}}_{\hskip-57.36131pt\|\mathrm{vec}(\mathcal{E}_{S,G_{k}^{\ast}})\|_{\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}^{2}\lesssim},\quad\underbrace{\frac{\zeta^{S}_{N,kl}}{\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}}}_{\hskip-24.58342pt\sup_{\mathbf{M}\in\mathcal{A}^{S}_{kl}}\|\mathbf{M}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim}\biggr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}}}.

The proof is complete once we show that the intersection event

\bigcap_{S\in[p],|S|\leq\sqrt{p}}\bigcap_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\hskip-10.00002pt\mathcal{C}_{N,kl}^{S}

holds with high probability.

5. Uniform bound.

Now we show that the event
\mathcal{C}_{N,kl}^{S}
holds uniformly across all
S\in[p]
such that
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
and
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
, with probability at least
1-C_{11}/n
, where
C_{11}>0
is a constant.

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\hskip-10.00002pt\bigcup_{S\in[p],|S|\leq\sqrt{p}}\bigcup_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\hskip-10.00002pt(\mathcal{C}_{N,kl}^{S})^{C}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\hskip-3.99994pt\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\mathbb{P}((\mathcal{C}_{N,kl}^{S})^{C})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\frac{K^{2}}{n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\frac{K^{2}}{nq^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{2}\pi^{2}}{6n},

where the last
step uses

\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}=\pi^{2}/6.

Since
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
,
|S|\log p
dominates
\log|S|
. Also, we treat
K
as a constant. Therefore, we conclude we conclude that
uniformly across
S\in\mathcal{S}
and
(k,l)
such that
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
,
with probability at least
1-C_{11}/n
, it holds that

\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\hskip-10.00002ptG^{S}_{1,kl}(\mathbf{v})\lesssim|G_{l}^{\ast}|\sqrt{|S|(\log n+|S|\log p)}

This completes the proof of Lemma
25
.

∎

C.4.12
Proof of Lemma
26

The proof proceeds in the following steps.

1.

Conditional analysis using a concentration inequality for the supremum of a Gaussian process,

2.

Individual bound by Chi-square tail bound,

3.

Uniform bound

Proof.

For notational simplicity, let
\sigma^{2}=1
.

1. Conditional analysis.

Since the sum of inner products of Gaussian random vectors is challenging to analyze directly, we first condition on
\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}
and then apply the concentration inequality for the supremum of a Gaussian process (Lemma
9
).
Fix
(k,l)
such that
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
and
S\subset[p]
such that
|S|\leq\sqrt{p}
.
Conditioned on
\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}
,
G^{S}_{2,kl}(\mathbf{v})
can be viewed as a canonical Gaussian process
\langle G,\mathbf{w}\rangle
, where
G\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{|G_{k}^{\ast}|})
and
\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{W}_{k}
, with

\mathbb{W}_{k}:=\left\{\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}\mathbf{v}_{\upharpoonright G_{k}^{\ast}}:\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1\right\},

where
\mathbf{v}_{\upharpoonright G_{k}^{*}}
is the restriction of
\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K}
onto
G_{k}^{\ast}
, and the canonical metric on
\mathbb{W}_{k}
is the
\ell_{2}
-metric.

To apply Lemma
9
, we have to bound

\displaystyle\mathrm{EC}_{kl}^{S}(\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast})

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}v_{i}\bigl\langle\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\bigr\rangle\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\bigr]=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{W}_{k}}\langle G,\mathbf{w}\rangle\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\bigr]~\text{, and}

\displaystyle\mathrm{SVC}_{kl}^{S}(\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast})

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{k},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{*}}v_{i}\bigl\langle\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast},\mathcal{E}_{S,i}\bigr\rangle\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\bigr)=\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{W}_{k}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\langle G,\mathbf{w}\rangle\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\bigr).

We bound
\mathrm{EC}_{kl}^{S}(\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast})
using Dudley’s integral inequality (Lemma
10
):

\displaystyle\mathrm{EC}_{kl}^{S}(\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast})\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{\log N(\mathbb{W}_{k},\epsilon)}\,d\epsilon

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|\log(1/\varepsilon)}\,d\varepsilon

\displaystyle=\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|},

where step
(i)
uses the fact that the
\varepsilon
-covering entropy of the unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}
is at most
|G_{k}^{\ast}|\log(1/\varepsilon)
, up to a constant factor, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.

Next, we bound
\mathrm{SVC}_{kl}^{S}(\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast})
:

\mathrm{SVC}_{kl}^{S}(\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast})=\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{W}_{k}}\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\langle G,\mathbf{w}\rangle\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\bigr)=\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{W}_{k}}\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{i\in G_{k}^{\ast}}v_{i}^{2}\leq\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2},

where the last inequality follows from
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1
for
\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K}
.
Let us denote
\xi^{S}=\log(n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}})
.
Define the following conditional probability
as follows:

\mathrm{CP}_{N,kl}^{S}:=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}|G_{2,kl}^{S}(\mathbf{v})|\gtrsim|G_{l}^{\ast}|\underbrace{\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|}}_{\gtrsim\mathrm{EC}_{kl}^{S}(\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast})}+|G_{l}^{\ast}|\underbrace{\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}}_{\gtrsim\sqrt{\mathrm{SVC}_{kl}^{S}(\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}})}\sqrt{\xi^{S}}\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\biggr),

Then by Lemma
9
,
we have

\mathrm{CP}_{N,kl}^{S}\leq\frac{1}{n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}}}.

(63)

2. Chi-square tail bound

Now we use the Chi-square tail inequality to further bound

\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}
and
\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}
.
If we define the following event:

\mathcal{B}_{N,kl}^{S}:=\left\{\|\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\left(|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\xi^{S}}+2\xi^{S}\right)\right\}.

By Lemma
3
, we have

\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{B}_{N,kl}^{S})^{C}\bigr)\leq\frac{1}{n|S|^{2}{\binom{p}{|S|}}}.

Combining the bounds in
\mathrm{CP}_{N,kl}^{S}
and
\mathcal{B}_{N,kl}^{S}
, we define an event:

\mathcal{A}_{N,kl}^{S}:=\biggl\{\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}|G_{2,kl}^{S}(\mathbf{v})|\lesssim\sqrt{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\sqrt{|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\xi^{S}}+2\xi^{S}}(\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+\sqrt{\xi^{S}})}\biggr\}.

The proof is complete once we show that the intersection event

\bigcap_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\bigcap_{S\in[p],|S|\leq\sqrt{p}}\mathcal{A}^{S}_{N,kl}

holds with high probability.

3. Uniform bound.

We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\biggl(\bigcup_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}

\displaystyle\bigcup_{S\in[p],|S|\leq\sqrt{p}}{(\mathcal{A}^{S}_{N,kl})^{C}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\mathbb{P}{\bigl((\mathcal{A}^{S}_{N,kl})^{C}\bigr)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{A}^{S}_{N,kl})^{C}\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathbb{P}\bigl((\mathcal{A}^{S}_{N,kl})^{C}\;|\;\overline{\mathcal{E}}_{S,l}^{\ast}\bigr)\mathbbm{1}(\mathcal{B}^{S}_{N,kl})+\mathbbm{1}\bigl((\mathcal{B}^{S}_{N,kl})^{C}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}\left(\sum_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\sum_{q=1}^{\lfloor\sqrt{p}\rfloor}\sum_{|S|=q}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\mathrm{CP}_{N,kl}^{S}\;\mathbbm{1}(\mathcal{B}^{S}_{N,kl})\bigr]\right)+\frac{K^{2}\pi^{2}}{6n}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}\frac{K^{2}\pi^{2}}{6n}+\frac{K^{2}\pi^{2}}{6n},

where step
(i)
uses
\sum_{q=1}^{\infty}(1/q^{2})=\pi^{2}/6
, and step
(ii)
also uses it and additionally uses (
63
).

Treating
K
as a constant, we conclude that uniformly across
S\in\mathcal{S}
and
(k,l)
such that
1\leq k\neq l\leq K
,
with probability at least
1-C_{12}/n
, where
C_{12}>0
is a constant, it holds that

\sup_{\mathbf{v}\in\Gamma_{K},\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}=1}|G_{2,kl}^{S}(\mathbf{v})|\lesssim\sqrt{|G_{l}^{\ast}|}\sqrt{|S|+2\sqrt{|S|\xi^{S}}+2\xi^{S}}\sqrt{|G_{k}^{\ast}|+\sqrt{\xi^{S}}}.

This completes the proof of Lemma
26
.
∎

C.4.13
Proof of Lemma
27

Leveraging the decomposition of
T_{2,kl}^{S}
into
G_{1,kl}^{S}
and
G_{2,kl}^{S}
given in Appendix
C.3.3
, the proof proceeds in the following intermediate steps:

1.

Problem decomposition

2.

Inequality for the
G_{1}\times G_{1}
term

3.

Inequality for the
G_{2}\times G_{2}
term

4.

Conclusion

Proof.

For notational simplicity, let
\sigma^{2}=1
.

1. Problem decomposition.

Recall that we defined

\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}=\min_{1\leq k\neq l\leq K}\|(\boldsymbol{\mu}_{l}^{\ast}-\boldsymbol{\mu}_{k}^{\ast})_{S\cap S_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}
in Theorem
1
, and

\dot{\lambda}^{S}=|S|+m\Delta_{S\cap S_{0}}^{2}/4
in Lemma
21
.
